Created the [final design specification](./FINAL_DESIGN_SPECIFICATION.md).

It preserves Gemini’s nested spatial model and bundled relationships, with a quieter charcoal, stone and muted-accent palette. It covers navigation, evidence tracing, backend architecture, MCP contracts and acceptance criteria, while clearly separating established techniques from experimental mathematical claims.

> Review `FINAL_DESIGN_SPECIFICATION.md`. The product direction, neutral visual system, epistemic safeguards, and verification criteria (C1–C14) are approved. However, before you begin writing application code, you must expand Sections 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 13, 17, and 18 from high-level prose into concrete, implementation-ready engineering specifications.
>
> Update `FINAL_DESIGN_SPECIFICATION.md` (or generate a companion `TECHNICAL_IMPLEMENTATION_BLUEPRINT.md`) to lock down the following six areas with exact schemas, equations, and algorithms:
>
> ### 1. Lock Down the Remaining Decisions in Section 18
> Resolve the open choices in Section 18 with concrete baseline selections:
> - **Primary Language & Static Resolver:** Python 3.11+ using standard `ast` + `symtable` + import resolution for exact intra-repo/cross-repo Python analysis, paired with `tree-sitter` as the extensible multi-language fallback.
> - **Conceptual Architecture Rule Format:** Define a strict YAML/TOML schema (`geographer.rules.yaml`) supporting `version`, `world_policy` (`open` | `closed`), `components` (path glob / qualified-name regex mappings), and `rules` (`require`, `allow`, `forbid` with `source`, `target`, and `rationale`).
> - **Tokenizer & MCP Transport:** Use `tiktoken` (`o200k_base` / `cl100k_base`) for deterministic local token counting (with a documented UTF-8 byte/4 fallback only if offline weights are missing), served over standard MCP `stdio` and Streamable HTTP/SSE transports.
>
> ### 2. Restore Executable SQLite DDL & Indexing Contracts (Section 10)
> Keep the conceptual table in Section 10, and add the exact SQLite DDL (`CREATE TABLE` and `CREATE INDEX` statements with strict `CHECK` constraints and foreign keys) for:
> - `snapshots` (snapshot_id, repo_id, revision, digest, extractor_version, parse_failures_json)
> - `entities` (entity_id, snapshot_id, repo_id, sort, kind, language, qualified_name, rel_path, start_line, end_line, parent_id, depth, ancestors_binary_lifting_json, is_test, is_generated, is_vendor, is_external, ast_body_hash, wl_3hop_hash, qi_cluster_id)
> - `relationships` & `evidence` (supporting parallel typed edges, direction, resolution_status, confidence, and 1-to-many evidence records)
> - `documentary_incidences` (k-ary hyperedge table linking doc fragments to entity_ids without polluting dependency degree)
> - `quotient_projections` & `quotient_witness_map` (pre-computed rollups and cursor-pageable leaf witness mappings)
> - `level_scc_condensations` (per-level, per-filter SCC ID, component size, and longest-path DAG rank)
> - `layouts` (layout_version, seed, entity_id, center_x, center_y, center_z, radius)
>
> ### 3. Specify the Mixed-Detail Partition & Residual Group Algorithm (Section 8 & C4)
> Formalize the Mixed-Detail View partition required by Section 8 and Criterion C4:
> - Define how an active expansion state $E \subset V$ induces a disjoint partition $\Pi(E)$ of all leaf entities.
> - Specify how partially expanded containers generate an explicit synthetic **Residual Group** node $R_p$ representing $\text{Children}(p) \setminus E$, how active representatives $\text{rep}(u)$ are assigned so every leaf maps to exactly one active representative, and how internal self-edges ($\text{rep}(u) = \text{rep}(v)$) are counted and exposed in the inspector without double-counting on parent shells.
> - Include the exact binary-lifting LCA algorithm and tree-distance equation:
>   $$d_T(x, y) = \text{depth}(x) + \text{depth}(y) - 2 \cdot \text{depth}(\text{LCA}(x, y))$$
>   used to evaluate $\text{DOI}(x \mid y) = -(d_T(x, y) + \text{depth}(x)) \ge -\text{depth}(y) - 2r$.
>
> ### 4. Write the Explicit 3D Packing & B-Spline Geometry Math (Sections 7 & 9)
> Replace the high-level geometry descriptions with deterministic mathematical specifications:
> - **Bottom-Up Sphere Packing Solver:** Define the recursive radius formula from capped/log-scaled descendant counts, the deterministic Fibonacci spiral spherical placement for $K$ siblings inside parent radius $r_p$, and the bounded iterative collision-projection step enforcing $\text{dist}(c_i, c_p) + r_i \le r_p - \text{padding}$ and $\text{dist}(c_i, c_j) \ge r_i + r_j + \text{gap}$ within numerical tolerance $\epsilon = 10^{-4}$.
> - **Holten Control Polygon Straightening & Clamped Knot Vectors:** Include the exact Method 1 equation:
>   $$P'_i = \beta \cdot P_i + (1 - \beta)\left(P_0 + \frac{i}{N - 1}(P_{N - 1} - P_0)\right)$$
>   over clamped uniform knot vectors of degree $p = \min(3, N - 1)$, enforcing the $N > 3$ LCA removal rule.
> - **Parallel / Opposing Track Separation & EdgeLens:** Define the deterministic normal/binormal offset vector calculation so opposing curves ($A \to B$ vs. $B \to A$) and parallel relation families between the same endpoints never z-fight or coincide in 3D space. Include the spatial radial falloff function for local unbundling around a selected node or cursor.
>
> ### 5. Integrate Well-Scoped Mathematical Diagnostics into the Core Pipeline (Sections 11 & 17)
> Keep the honest naming and epistemic guardrails from Section 11, but move the deterministic mathematical computations from "Stage 5 Research Extensions" into the core Stage 1–3 pipeline so both the Advanced Inspector and MCP tools have real computed values:
> - **Exact AST Normalization + 3-Hop Weisfeiler-Lehman (WL) Hashing:** Specify the exact AST normalization rules (stripping docstrings/comments, canonicalizing local variable identifiers vs. preserving calls) and the 3-hop WL neighborhood signature hash for `find_similar_code`.
> - **Boundary Cut Ratio (Cheeger-style Subgraph Metric):** Specify the directed (incoming/outgoing) and undirected weighted boundary ratio $\phi(S) = |\partial S| / \text{vol}(S)$ for any selected container or subsystem $S$.
> - **Quasi-Isometric (QI) Bounded-Diameter Contraction:** Specify the parallel BFS ball-contraction algorithm that collapses local helper trees of diameter $\le B$ into summary representatives for budgeted `get_architecture_context` calls, while explicitly reporting path-distortion bounds $(A, B)$ and witness counts.
> - **Abelian Cayley Degree Asymmetry Bound (Research Diagnostic):** Include Souop & Bitjoka's $O(n)$ lower bound $\gamma^+(G) \ge \frac{n\Delta^*}{2m} - 1$ on the simple undirected projection of a subsystem graph (when $n \ge 2, m \ge 1$) inside the collapsible Advanced Diagnostics section, explicitly logging removed self-loops and collapsed parallel edges as required by Section 11.
>
> ### 6. Define Full Wire Schemas & Token-Packing Algorithm for Section 13
> For all 7 shared query/MCP operations (`search_entities`, `get_architecture_context`, `inspect_relationship_witnesses`, `get_entity_content`, `find_dependants`, `check_architecture`, `find_similar_code`) and the 3D viewport endpoint:
> - Provide the exact JSON/TypeScript request and response interfaces (`NodeRecord`, `RelationshipSummary`, `WitnessRecord`, `EvidenceHandle`, `ConformanceFinding`, `ErrorEnvelope`).
> - Write the step-by-step greedy knapsack/priority packing algorithm that guarantees `used_tokens <= limit_tokens` and enforces the invariant that no relationship is ever serialized without both of its endpoint node definitions present in `nodes`.
>

> here is a review and gap analysis of your work up to now, please read it then present an implementation plan : ""### 1. Executive Review of Codex’s Progress
>
> Codex has executed Stages 1 through 4 and a full UI/UX QA remediation pass with a very high level of engineering rigor:
>
> * **Contract & Data Integrity (Stages 1–2):** Codex implemented the 14-table `STRICT` SQLite store with runtime immutability triggers (`store.py`), `scope_atom` leaf normalization ($T^+$) (`model.py`), `symtable` + CFG fixed-point binding analysis (`scopes.py`, `cfg.py`), `py-ast-v1` structural normalization (`normalize.py`), mixed-detail partitions $\Pi(E)$ with residual groups $R_p$ (`analysis.py`), iterative Tarjan SCC condensation (`analysis.py`), 3-round synchronous Weisfeiler-Lehman hashing (`analysis.py`), and degree-volume boundary cut ratios alongside Souop & Bitjoka’s abelian Cayley degree lower bounds (`analysis.py`).
>
> * **MCP & Token Packing (Stage 3):** Codex auto-generates JSON Schemas and strict Pydantic v2 models directly from `contracts/geographer.wire.d.ts` (`generate-contracts.cjs`, `wire.py`), vendors offline `tiktoken` weights (`o200k_base` and `cl100k_base`) (`tokens.py`), enforces endpoint-closed greedy packing (`packing.py`), and serves identical responses across local HTTP and MCP (`stdio` and Streamable HTTP `/mcp` pinned to `2025-11-25`) (`server.py`, `service.py`).
>
> * **3D Workspace & QA Hardening (Stage 4 + QA):** Codex built the `sphere-pack-v1` solver with the requested transverse helical line-fallback stagger (`geometry.py`), Greville-projected clamped B-spline lanes and EdgeLens (`geometry.py`, `curves.ts`), and a synchronized React + Three.js + Hierarchy + Matrix interface (`App.tsx`, `Scene.tsx`, `Hierarchy.tsx`, `Matrix.tsx`, `Inspector.tsx`). It also found and fixed 13 subtle UI/state bugs during Playwright QA, passing 71 backend tests and 26 browser tests with zero `axe` accessibility violations and stable GPU memory over a 10-minute soak test.
>
> ---
>
> ### 2. Current State vs. Envisioned System (Code-Level Gap Analysis)
>
> While the system’s **structural invariants and safety guardrails** are rock-solid, inspecting the implementation in `src/geographer/` and `frontend/src/` reveals five material gaps between the current build and the envisioned daily-driver tool described in `FINAL_DESIGN_SPECIFICATION_2.md` and `TECHNICAL_IMPLEMENTATION_BLUEPRINT.md`:
>
> #### Gap 1: 84.9% of Real Python Relationships Are Unresolved or Hidden by Default (`extract.py` & `scopes.py`)
>
> In `STAGE_1_GATE_REPORT.md` and `STAGE_4_GATE_REPORT.md`, ingesting your real `ToolSet` repository produced 12,526 relationships, but **10,629 (84.9%) were marked `unresolved**`, 395 (3.2%) were marked `candidate`, and only **1,502 (12.0%) were marked `exact**`. Because `DEFAULT_FILTER` in `analysis.py` and `api.ts` filters strictly to `resolution_statuses: ["exact"]`, **88% of your repository's call/dependency graph is invisible by default** in both the 3D scene and MCP `get_architecture_context` queries.
>
> Inspecting `extract.py` and `scopes.py` shows four specific reasons why intra-repository resolution is overly pessimistic:
>
> 1. **`scope.complex_flow` blanket downgrade (`scopes.py` line 117 & `extract.py` line 251):** In `ScopeIndex._protect()`, if a function contains *any* `try/except`, `match`, `:=`, `await`, or `yield` statement anywhere in its body, `scope.complex_flow` is set to `True`. In `PythonExtractor.call()`, `uncertain = force_uncertain or scope.reflective or scope.complex_flow` downgrades **every single call inside that function to `candidate**`—even calls to unambiguously imported top-level functions that are never reassigned. In modern Python (especially `async def` FastAPI/MCP codebases and functions with `try` blocks), this disqualifies the vast majority of functions from having `exact` calls.
>
> 2. **Broken `from pkg import module_or_class` member calls (`extract.py` lines 162, 256–260):** In `index_bindings()`, `from a.b import c` is always indexed with `binding.kind = "member"`. However, in `call()`, when resolving a dotted call like `c.do_work()` (`parts = ["do_work"]`), line 256 checks `if parts and binding.kind == "module":` and completely ignores `binding.kind == "member"`! Thus, `from aletheia_toolchain import utils; utils.run()` or `from .models import Store; Store.open()` always falls through to `unresolved`.
>
> 3. **Zero support for `self.method()`, `cls.method()`, and class inheritance (`extract.py`):** `PythonExtractor` never extracts class inheritance edges (`ClassDef.bases`) and never resolves `self.<method>()` or `cls.<method>()` inside class methods (even as `static_candidate` or `exact` when a method is not overridden), turning every object-oriented method call into an `unresolved` external placeholder.
>
> 4. **Unbound module-level forward calls (`extract.py` line 213):** Because `initial` state only seeds parameters, module-level top-level function definitions called inside another function can be marked uncertain if module bindings aren't recognized as single-assignment definitions.
>
> #### Gap 2: 13.1-Second Cold Expanded Viewport Latency (`packing.py`, `analysis.py`, & `service.py`)
>
> In `STAGE_4_GATE_REPORT.md`, Codex reported that loading an expanded viewport on the 50k-entity / 150k-edge benchmark took **13,133 ms (13.1 seconds)** on a cold plan, forcing Codex to work around the 3-second startup target by booting into a collapsed 2-sphere view (`contextRadius = 0`).
>
> Inspecting `packing.py`, `analysis.py`, and `service.py` reveals two severe algorithmic bottlenecks causing this 13-second stall:
>
> 1. **Row-by-row SQLite inserts in Python loops (`analysis.py` lines 253–264 & `service.py` lines 199–208):** While `store.write_graph()` uses `executemany`, both `materialize_view()` and `QueryService._persist_partition()` insert 50,000 `partition_members` rows and up to 150,000 `quotient_witness_map` rows one row at a time via `self.store._insert(...)` inside nested Python `for` loops.
>
> 2. **$O(K^2)$ Full-Envelope Deepcopy, JSON Serialization, and BPE Tokenization (`packing.py` lines 48–137):** Inside `GreedyPacker.pack()`, the `while heap and len(accepted) < page_size:` loop pops up to 200 candidate units one by one. On *every single candidate test*, it calls `finalise(accepted + [candidate])`, which runs `deepcopy` on every accepted record in `combine()`, re-sorts all arrays, serializes the entire growing JSON envelope twice (`canonical(region)` and `canonical(envelope)`), SHA-256 hashes the full string, and runs `tiktoken.encode_ordinary()` on the entire multi-kilobyte JSON payload 200+ times in a row. Incremental token estimation with periodic/final exact verification (or binary-search batching) will cut this from seconds to milliseconds.
>
> #### Gap 3: Missing 3D Visual Semantic Encodings (`Scene.tsx` vs. Product Spec §6)
>
> Product Spec §6 ("Relationship encoding") specifies distinct visual treatments in the 3D scene for ordinary dependencies, runtime observations (`#9FB7AC` sage), expected-only links (dashed), rule violations (`#D5B27B` warning amber / `#D3958E` critical red), and inferred/ambiguous relations (dotted).
>
> In `Scene.tsx` (lines 118–126 and 286–305):
>
> * All unselected curves share a single `THREE.LineBasicMaterial({ color: 0xa8adb4 })` without per-vertex color attributes (`vertexColors: true`).
>
> * Only the `dotted()` check for `candidate`/`unresolved` is implemented (by skipping odd segments); **rule violations, runtime observations, relation subtypes (`imports` vs. `calls`), and directional source $\to$ target color/taper cues on unselected bundles are not visually differentiated in 3D**.
>
> * In addition, `Scene.tsx` only shows the directional arrow (`THREE.ArrowHelper`) when a single edge is explicitly selected, and does not support hover-highlighting of curves or endpoints.
>
> #### Gap 4: Missing UI Controls Specified in Product Spec §§5, 8, & 11 (`App.tsx`, `Inspector.tsx`, `viewport.py`)
>
> 1. **Missing Second-Order Fisheye (`contextRadius = 2` / "Wider"):** Product Spec §8 and Blueprint §3.2/§7.1 define $r \in \{0, 1, 2\}$ (`Path`, `Nearby`, `Wider`), and `TreeIndex.context()` in `analysis.py` supports `radius in (0, 1, 2)`. However, `App.tsx` restricts `contextRadius` to `0 | 1` (`useState<0 | 1>(0)`) and `validSaved` in `api.ts` rejects `2`.
>
> 2. **Missing Abstraction Level / Grouping Selector in the Workspace:** Product Spec §5 and §8 specify an abstraction selector (`Repository`, `Directory/Subsystem`, `File`, `Class`, `Atomic`, and `Conceptual` component enclosures). While `service.py` implements all of these grouping modes for MCP (`_architecture`), `viewport.py` hardcodes `grouping = {'mode': 'mixed', ...}` and the UI has no control to switch abstraction modes or view Conceptual Component enclosures in 3D/Matrix.
>
> 3. **Missing Outgoing Dependency Traversal & Subtype Filters in UI:** In `Inspector.tsx` (line 232), `dependants()` hardcodes `direction: "incoming"`. Users cannot click a button to trace `outgoing` transitive dependencies (callees / what this symbol depends on), nor can they filter by relation `subtypes` (`calls`, `imports`, `imports_from`, `inherits`) in the UI filter popover (`App.tsx` lines 660–715).
>
> 4. **No UI/API Workflow to Load or Switch `geographer.rules.yaml`:** If the server wasn't started with `--rules`, the Inspector says *"No architecture rules configured"* with no way to upload, paste, or select a rules file in the workspace.
>
> #### Gap 5: Missing Runtime Trace Ingestion Pipeline (`cli.py` / `pipeline.py`)
>
> The database schema (`store.py`), query filters (`analysis.py`), and MCP contracts (`service.py`) all have built-in support for `family='runtime'` and `category='dynamic_observed'` with collection time windows, but **there is no extractor or CLI subcommand to actually ingest runtime traces** (e.g., from `sys.monitoring` / `trace` / `coverage` or OpenTelemetry span JSONL files) into a snapshot. As a result, `telemetry_available` is always `false` on real repositories, and the `"Runtime overlay unavailable"` button in `App.tsx` is permanently disabled.
>
> ---
>
> ### 3. Direction & Long-Term Goals for Codex
>
> To transition Geo Grapher from a contract-verified prototype into a **high-utility, daily-driver architectural platform for both human engineers and LLM coding agents**, Codex should now execute a structured **Phase 5 Completion & Optimization Plan** focused on five pillars:
>
> 1. **High-Recall, Epistemically Honest Python Resolution:** Separate AST control-flow complexity from immutable module/import bindings, resolve `from pkg import mod_or_cls` member calls, resolve `self`/`cls` method calls and class inheritance (`inherits`), and make the default view show verified `exact` + high-confidence `static_candidate` intra-repo calls (clearly badged/dotted) so real repositories like `ToolSet` render rich, connected architecture graphs out of the box.
>
> 2. **Sub-Second Cold Viewport & Query Performance:** Replace row-by-row SQLite `_insert` loops in view materialization with batched `executemany`, and optimize `GreedyPacker` to use incremental token delta accounting with a final exact-envelope verification pass—bringing the 13.1s cold expanded viewport under the 3.0s C14 target.
>
> 3. **Complete UI & 3D Visual Fidelity:** Expose `r = 0, 1, 2` (`Path`, `Nearby`, `Wider`) context breadth, abstraction level modes (including Conceptual Architecture enclosures), `incoming` vs. `outgoing` dependency impact traversal, subtype filters, per-vertex 3D curve color encodings (violations, runtime observations, subtypes, hover highlights), and live `geographer.rules.yaml` loading.
>
> 4. **Runtime Telemetry Ingestion CLI & Overlay:** Add a `geographer ingest-trace` CLI command (and pytest/coverage hook) that records or imports real Python call traces (`sys.monitoring` / JSONL spans) into `family='runtime'` relationships so users and LLMs can compare static predictions against runtime reality.
>
> 5. **Automated LLM vs. Baseline Evaluation Suite:** Build a reproducible evaluation script to close the comparative task benchmark in `RELEASE_ACCEPTANCE.md`.
>
> ---

>
> Review your current implementation across `src/geographer/`, `frontend/src/`, and the open items in `STAGE_4_GATE_REPORT.md` and `RELEASE_ACCEPTANCE.md`.
>
> Your Stage 1–4 contract implementation and Playwright QA remediation pass are excellent. However, before we run final release acceptance, a code audit identified five concrete gaps between the current build and our product vision that limit real-world usefulness on repositories like `ToolSet`:
>
> ### 1. Python Resolver Recall & Intra-Repo Connectivity (`extract.py`, `scopes.py`)
>
> On `ToolSet`, 10,629 of 12,526 relationships (84.9%) are currently `unresolved` and only 12.0% are `exact`. Because `DEFAULT_FILTER` only includes `exact`, 88% of the repository's relationships are hidden by default. Fix the following four resolver gaps while preserving honest provenance (`exact` vs. `candidate` vs. `unresolved`):
>
> - **Decouple `scope.complex_flow` from invariant bindings (`scopes.py` & `extract.py`):** Currently, `ScopeIndex._protect()` sets `scope.complex_flow = True` if a function contains *any* `try`, `match`, `:=`, `await`, or `yield`, and `PythonExtractor.call()` forces `uncertain = True` for *every* call in that function. Change this so `complex_flow` only downgrades bindings that are actually reassigned/mutated across branches in the local CFG; top-level imports, module-level single-assignment functions/classes, and unmutated lexical bindings called inside `async def` or `try` blocks must remain `exact`.
>
> - **Resolve `from pkg import submod_or_class` dotted calls (`extract.py`):** In `index_bindings()`, `ImportFrom` bindings are assigned `kind = "member"`. In `call()`, when `parts` is non-empty (e.g., `utils.helper()` or `MyClass.build()`), line 256 only checks `if parts and binding.kind == "module":`, completely skipping `binding.kind == "member"`. Extend `call()` so `binding.kind == "member"` resolves `self.resolve_member(binding.module, [binding.member, *parts], {bid})`.
>
> - **Extract Class Inheritance & `self`/`cls` Method Calls (`extract.py`):**
>
>   - Emit directed `inherits` relationships (`subtype = "inherits"`) from `ast.ClassDef` to resolved base classes in `node.bases`.
>
>   - Inside methods of a `ClassDef`, resolve `self.<attr>()` and `cls.<attr>()` against the enclosing class and its resolved base-class hierarchy (marking as `exact` when uniquely defined on a final/unsubclassed class in the snapshot with no dynamic attribute mutation, or `candidate` when polymorphic/overridden).
>
> - **Default Filter Visibility:** Update the default UI filter (or provide a prominent 1-click preset in the top bar) so intra-repository `candidate` edges are easy to view alongside `exact` edges (distinguished visually by dotted lines and badges), while keeping noisy unresolved external symbols filtered out when desired.
>
> ### 2. Fix the 13.1s Cold Expanded Viewport Bottleneck (`packing.py`, `analysis.py`, `service.py`)
>
> In `STAGE_4_GATE_REPORT.md`, the cold expanded viewport took 13,133 ms on the 50k/150k benchmark (missing the <3,000 ms C14 target). Fix the two root causes in the backend:
>
> - **Batch View Persistence (`analysis.py` & `service.py`):** In `materialize_view()` and `_persist_partition()`, replace the Python `for` loops calling `self.store._insert(...)` 200,000+ times on `view_representatives`, `partition_members`, `quotient_projections`, `quotient_witness_map`, and `level_scc_condensations` with batched `connection.executemany(...)` calls.
>
> - **Eliminate $O(K^2)$ Full-Envelope Re-Serialization & BPE Re-Tokenization (`packing.py`):** In `GreedyPacker.pack()`, popping 200 candidates currently runs `finalise(accepted + [candidate])` 200+ times—executing `deepcopy`, full JSON serialization, SHA-256, and `tiktoken.encode_ordinary()` on the entire growing envelope on every iteration. Refactor `GreedyPacker.pack()` to maintain mutable working collections during the greedy loop, track incremental token costs per newly added record (with a safety margin for JSON punctuation), and run full-envelope `finalise()` + exact `tiktoken` verification only when approaching the budget boundary and at final commit (followed by the existing non-monotonic eviction guard). Re-benchmark cold expanded viewport latency on the 50k/150k dataset and record the speedup.
>
> ### 3. Complete Missing UI Controls & 3D Visual Encodings (`App.tsx`, `Inspector.tsx`, `Scene.tsx`, `viewport.py`)
>
> Bring the workspace UI into full parity with `FINAL_DESIGN_SPECIFICATION.md` §§5, 6, 8, and 11:
>
> - **Context Breadth (`r = 0, 1, 2`):** Replace the binary `0 | 1` toggle in `App.tsx` (and `validSaved` in `api.ts`) with a 3-state segmented control for `context_radius`: **Path ($r=0$)**, **Nearby ($r=1$)**, and **Wider ($r=2$)**.
>
> - **Grouping / Abstraction Mode Selector:** Allow the user to switch the workspace view between **Interactive Mixed**, **Subsystems / Directories**, **Files**, **Classes**, and **Conceptual Components** (when a rules model is active).
>
> - **Bidirectional Impact & Subtype Filters:**
>
>   - In `Inspector.tsx`, allow the user to toggle between **Incoming dependants (callers/importers)** and **Outgoing dependencies (callees/imports)** when running `find_dependants`, and offer a button to highlight/pin the discovered witness path in the 3D scene.
>
>   - In `App.tsx` (`Scope & filters`), add checkboxes for relationship `subtypes` (`calls`, `imports`, `imports_from`, `inherits`) and `families` (`static`, `runtime` when available).
>
> - **Per-Curve 3D Visual Encodings & Hover Inspection (`Scene.tsx`):**
>
>   - Enable `vertexColors: true` on the curve `LineBasicMaterial` in `Scene.tsx` and encode relationship status using the approved neutral palette tokens (`#A8ADB4` ordinary, `#9FB7AC` runtime observed, `#D5B27B` warning / `#D3958E` critical when an edge witnesses a `forbidden_present` rule violation, and a subtle source $\to$ target brightness/tint ramp along each curve so direction is legible even before clicking).
>
>   - Add pointer-hover raycasting on spheres and curves to display a lightweight floating tooltip (`source → target · subtype · witness count`) without mutating the locked Inspector selection.
>
> - **Live Rules Configuration:** Add an action or CLI/UI endpoint to load or reload a `geographer.rules.yaml` file into an active workspace without rebuilding the snapshot.
>
> ### 4. Implement Runtime Trace Ingestion (`cli.py`, `pipeline.py`, `trace.py`)
>
> The schema, store validator, and query engine already support `family='runtime'` and `category='dynamic_observed'` with `runtime_window`, but there is no way to ingest runtime data:
>
> - Add a `geographer ingest-trace` CLI command (and Python API) that accepts a completed `snapshot_id`, an environment name, a collection window (`start`, `end`), and a trace file (supporting a clean JSONL call-trace format `{"caller_path", "caller_qualname", "caller_line", "callee_path", "callee_qualname", "call_count", "p95_ms"}` plus a helper wrapper using Python 3.12+/3.11 `sys.setprofile` to record a command or pytest run directly).
>
> - Map observed calls onto the snapshot's canonical entities, attach `dynamic_observed` evidence, and enable the **Runtime overlay** toggle and `runtime_window` selector in the UI when runtime facts exist in the snapshot.
>
> ### 5. First Deliverables

> ```""

Written: [PHASE_5_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md](./PHASE_5_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md).

The sequence is:

1. Establish profiling, recall and contract baselines.
2. Improve Python resolution without overstating certainty.
3. Batch persistence and optimise packing while preserving budget guarantees.
4. Complete grouping, traversal, rules and visual controls.
5. Add runtime tracing through immutable derived snapshots.
6. Build the comparative evaluation harness and reassess release gates.

The plan identifies necessary contract extensions, regression tests and measurable exit criteria. No application code or approved contracts were changed.

> Here is the final review pass, this time focused on making sure we develop a system that is both informative and token efficient for any actor using it. 1. Review of Codex’s Revised Plan (PHASE_5_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_2.md)
> Codex’s updated plan (§§1–25) is a comprehensive, epistemically disciplined engineering blueprint. It integrates the findings from the research audit and technical expansions into the existing Phase 5 schedule (P5.0–P5.5) without breaking the verified v1.0 contracts:
>  * Orthogonal Tree Topologies (T^+, T_D, and T_S): Codex cleanly separates the canonical code containment tree (T^+) from the new Markdown/docstring section hierarchy (T_D, documentary-tree-v1) and the intra-file AST statement/clause tree (T_S, source-context-v1). This guarantees that adding documentation sections or statement-level code folding never alters canonical code ownership, dependency degree, or SCC/WL metrics.
>  * Four-Part Claim & Reason Registry (claim-details-v1): Section 19 formalizes the exact boundary between syntax, static_conclusion, runtime_observation, and documentary_assertion, attaching deterministic occurrence_ids and a closed registry of reason_codes (LEXICAL_UNIQUE_BINDING, STATIC_MRO_UNIQUE, POLYMORPHIC_MRO_CANDIDATE, EXTERNAL_PACKAGE_UNINDEXED, etc.).
>  * Two-Channel Local Retrieval (lexical-fusion-v1): Section 20 keeps exact symbol lookup as an untouchable Tier 1 result so prose never outranks an exact identifier match, then applies Reciprocal Rank Fusion (\operatorname{RRF}) across a camelCase/snake_case Formal Code Channel and an FTS5 BM25 Informal Documentary Channel.
>  * Finite-State Typed Witness Bridges (typed-bridge-v1): Section 21 models multi-hop path extraction between focus and pinned anchors as a bounded BFS over the product of the subject graph and a path-template FSM (CALL_CHAIN_WITH_HELPERS, INHERITANCE_AND_OVERRIDES, BOUNDARY_CROSSING_WITNESSES), with explicit per-pair completion outcomes (found, no_path_within_scope, hop_limit_reached, omitted_by_budget).
> 2. Diagnosing the Actor Schema Balance: Why Stage 3 Burned 8,132 Tokens for 7 Nodes & 8 Edges
> Your concern—that the forward-facing schema for an actor (human or LLM) must be neither too condensed to interpret nor too dense to be token-efficient—is the single most important architectural issue to lock down in P5.0.
> Look at the empirical measurement in STAGE_3_GATE_REPORT.md: a real ToolSet file-level get_architecture_context query returned only 7 node definitions and 8 relationships, yet consumed 8,132 tokens (over 500 tokens per item). Inspecting service.py reveals that the current v1.0 wire payload simultaneously suffers from both problems:
>  * Why it is token-inefficient (Cryptographic & Boilerplate Bloat):
>    * BPE-Hostile Hex Digests: Every NodeRecord in service.py serializes id (a 71-character SHA-256 string), entity_id (the exact same 71-character string repeated), parent_id (71 characters), ast_body_hash (64 characters), wl_3hop_hash (64 characters), membership_handle (78 characters), and source.revision (40-character Git SHA repeated on every node).
>    * Per-Edge HMAC Signatures: In service.py (_summary, line 127), every single RelationshipSummary embeds a witness_cursor containing a base64-encoded JSON payload + 256-bit HMAC signature (~300+ characters).
>    * In tiktoken (o200k_base), random hexadecimal and base64 strings cannot compress into word tokens; they shatter into 1 token every 2–3 characters. Roughly 70% of the token budget in get_architecture_context is currently spent on SHA-256 hashes and unused per-edge HMAC cursors rather than code semantics.
>  * Why it is too condensed to interpret (Opaque Black-Box Nodes & Edges):
>    * Despite spending ~500 tokens per node/edge, an actor looking at a NodeRecord for a function gets zero signature or parameter information and zero docstring summary—only its name and line numbers.
>    * An actor looking at a rolled-up group node (residual, qi, directory, or file) sees only "Other children of X", member_count: 38, and an opaque membership_handle. They have no idea what classes or functions are inside that group without issuing another tool call.
>    * An actor looking at a rolled-up RelationshipSummary (File A -> File B, calls, witness_count: 19) sees no preview of which functions actually call each other across that boundary, forcing an immediate round-trip call to inspect_relationship_witnesses.
> 3. The "Goldilocks" Forward-Facing Actor Schema (v1.1 Design)
> To ensure an actor at any zoom level has immediate semantic interpretability within a lean token footprint (~60–90 tokens per node/edge instead of ~500), instruct Codex to build the following four rules into the contracts/v1.1/ schema during P5.0:
> Rule 1: Strip Cryptographic Entropy & Redundant Boilerplate from Context Envelopes
>  * Drop Per-Edge Initial HMAC Cursors: inspect_relationship_witnesses already accepts (view_id, projection_id, cursor=null) to fetch the first page of witnesses. Remove witness_cursor from RelationshipSummary in get_architecture_context (or set it to null until page 2 of a witness query), saving ~80–120 tokens per relationship.
>  * Compact Short-ID Alias Table (short_id): Assign deterministic, snapshot-scoped short aliases (n1, n2, g1, p1) or human-readable repo:rel_path#qualname keys inside the response envelope, mapping them once to full 64-byte SHA-256 canonical IDs if needed. Never repeat entity_id when it is identical to id.
>  * Scope-Level Revision & On-Demand Fingerprints: Omit source.revision on individual nodes when it matches the top-level revisions manifest for repo_id, and omit ast_body_hash / wl_3hop_hash from get_architecture_context (keep them in find_similar_code and diagnostics).
> Rule 2: Add "Semantic Micro-Descriptors" to Every Node (node.summary)
> Every node returned to an actor must carry a bounded, self-sufficient Micro-Descriptor (~20–35 tokens) tailored to whether it is an Atomic Entity or a Composite Group:
>  * For Atomic Entities (function, method, class, file):
>    * signature: The compact declaration header (e.g., (store: SnapshotStore, repos: list[Repository], *, stage: int = 1) -> dict or class QueryService).
>    * doc_gist: The first sentence (capped at 140 UTF-8 characters) of the entity's docstring or top section heading from T_D, with an explicit doc_handle if more exists.
>  * For Composite / Rolled-Up Groups (residual, qi, directory, package, conceptual):
>    * kind_counts: Breakdown of contained leaf kinds (e.g., {"file": 3, "class": 4, "function": 21}).
>    * exemplars: Up to 5 highest-degree or boundary-crossing member qualified_names (with their compact signatures) inside the group, so the actor immediately sees the group's primary entrypoints without expanding all 28 members.
> Rule 3: Add "Grounding Previews" to Rolled-Up Relationships (relationship.preview)
> When a RelationshipSummary aggregates W leaf relationships between two groups or files (A -> B), include a bounded Grounding Preview so the bundle is never an opaque number:
>  * top_witnesses: Up to 3 concrete leaf pairs (caller_qualname:line -> callee_qualname [reason_code], e.g., ["pipeline.ingest:24 -> extract.PythonExtractor.extract [LEXICAL_UNIQUE_BINDING]"]).
>  * reason_counts: Distribution of reason_codes across the W witnesses (e.g., {"LEXICAL_UNIQUE_BINDING": 14, "POLYMORPHIC_MRO_CANDIDATE": 2}).
>  * If W \le 3, the preview is the complete witness list (preview_complete: true), saving the LLM an entire round-trip tool call to inspect_relationship_witnesses!
> Rule 4: Furnas AST Skeleton Folding for source-context-v1 (T_S)
> When an actor calls get_entity_content in source-context-v1 mode (§25), prevent raw source dumps from blowing the context budget by structuring the output into three explicit tiers over T_S:
>  * Enclosing Spine Headers (r=0): Exact class / def / control-flow headers from the file root down to the target symbol, with omitted line intervals explicitly labeled (# ... lines 12-44 omitted [handle:...]).
>  * Focal Span: Complete verbatim source lines for the requested symbol or statement range.
>  * Sibling Stubs (r=1): 1-line signature + doc_gist + outgoing call targets for immediate sibling functions/methods (def _helper(x) -> int: ... # lines 90-118; calls: validate()), giving the actor complete local file orientation at 10% of the raw file token cost.
> 4. Prompt / Instructions to Give Codex
> Copy and paste the following prompt to Codex to approve PHASE_5_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md (§§1–25), lock in the Goldilocks Actor Schema for v1.1, and start execution on P5.0 and P5.1:
> Your updated `PHASE_5_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (§§1–25, including Workstream 6 and §25 `source-context-v1` / `process-evidence-v1`) is approved.
>
> As you execute **P5.0** and draft the additive `contracts/v1.1/` wire specification, enforce one overarching design constraint for the forward-facing schema served to actors (LLMs via MCP and humans in the Inspector): **the representation at any zoom level must never be so condensed that groups/edges become opaque black boxes, nor so dense with cryptographic/boilerplate metadata that it wastes the token budget.**
>
> Specifically, incorporate the following four **"Goldilocks Schema"** rules into `contracts/v1.1/` during **P5.0**:
>
> ### 1. Eliminate Cryptographic & Boilerplate Token Bloat (Target: ~5x More Graph Entities per Token Budget)
> In Stage 3 (`STAGE_3_GATE_REPORT.md`), returning just 7 nodes and 8 relationships consumed 8,132 tokens because ~70% of the BPE tokens were spent on 64-char SHA-256 hex strings and 300-char base64 HMAC cursors on every edge. In `v1.1`:
> - **Remove Per-Edge Initial HMAC Cursors:** Do not embed a pre-signed HMAC `witness_cursor` on every `RelationshipSummary` in `get_architecture_context` or `viewport_frame`. `inspect_relationship_witnesses` already accepts `(view_id, projection_id, cursor=null)` for page 1. Only emit signed continuation cursors when a collection is actively paginated (`next_cursor`).
> - **Deduplicate Identity & Revision Boilerplate:** In `v1.1` `NodeRecord`, do not repeat `entity_id` when it equals `id` (use a boolean `is_synthetic` or nullable `canonical_id` only when different), do not repeat 40-char Git `revision` strings on every node's `source` when already declared in the top-level `revisions` manifest, and omit `ast_body_hash` / `wl_3hop_hash` from `get_architecture_context` (serve them in `find_similar_code` and `diagnostics`).
>
> ### 2. Add Self-Sufficient "Semantic Micro-Descriptors" to Every Node (`v1.1` `NodeRecord`)
> Ensure an actor never sees an opaque node or group that forces blind drill-down calls:
> - **For Atomic Code Nodes (`function`, `method`, `class`, `file`):** Include `signature: string | null` (the extracted parameter/return signature or class bases header) and `doc_gist: string | null` (first sentence / <=140 chars of the docstring or top $T_D$ heading).
> - **For Composite / Rolled-Up Groups (`residual`, `qi`, `directory`, `package`, `conceptual`):** Alongside `member_count` and `membership_handle`, include `group_preview: { kind_counts: Record<string, number>; top_exemplars: { id: string; qualified_name: string; kind: string; signature: string | null }[] }` listing up to 5 highest-degree or boundary-crossing members inside that group.
>
> ### 3. Add "Witness Previews & Reason Breakdowns" to Rolled-Up Edges (`v1.1` `RelationshipSummary`)
> So an actor can interpret a high-level bundle (`U -> V`) without an immediate extra round-trip:
> - Add `witness_preview: { relationship_id: string; source_qualname: string; target_qualname: string; line: number | null; reason_code: string }[]` (up to 3 concrete leaf witnesses grounding the projection) and `reason_counts: Record<string, number>`.
> - Include `preview_complete: boolean` (`true` when `witness_count <= len(witness_preview)`), allowing the LLM or UI to skip calling `inspect_relationship_witnesses` when all witnesses are already visible in the preview.
>
> ### 4. Structured 3-Tier AST Folding in `source-context-v1` ($T_S$)
> When `get_entity_content` is called in `source-context-v1` mode (§25):
> - Return (1) the **Enclosing Spine Headers** (`class` / `def` / control-flow headers from module root to target with explicit omitted line spans), (2) the **Verbatim Focal Span**, and (3) **1-Line Sibling Stubs** (`signature` + `doc_gist` + outgoing call names + line range handle) for immediate siblings in $T_S$.
>
> ### Execution Kickoff
> Begin executing **Increment P5.0** and **Increment P5.1** now:
> 1. Produce the `P5.0` baseline call-site triage on `ToolSet`, the source/claim register, and the `contracts/v1.1/` wire declarations incorporating the Goldilocks Schema rules above.
> 2. Execute `P5.1` (Workstream 1 resolver upgrades: binding-specific certainty, recursive dotted `member` resolution, `inherits` + C3 MRO dispatch, `claim-details-v1` reason codes, and visibility presets).
> 3. Run the `P5.0` and `P5.1` test gates (including `KG1`) and report the before/after `ToolSet` call-site resolution and token-density metrics.

>
> ## Implemented changes
>
> - Binding-specific certainty removes the blanket complex-flow downgrade. Fixed-point CFG joins, local rebinding, expression-level walrus effects and exception-name cleanup remain conservative. Callee loading precedes argument-side writes. Known early module calls propagate through named local helpers and downgrade references to declarations not yet initialized.
> - Recursive imported-member traversal resolves modules, re-exports and nested class namespaces with cycle guards and export-before-submodule precedence. The package from-list case (`from . import submodule`) is handled explicitly. Assignment/deletion/setattr patches through module/member aliases invalidate affected exporters across files.
> - Source-grounded directed inheritance facts and a supported C3 class/member index are published. Inconsistent/dynamic/unindexed MROs are disclosed. Receiver bindings follow the first method parameter rather than the spelling self/cls; class/instance dispatch stays candidate under open-world assumptions. Noncallable class slots mask inherited methods; final annotations and lack of subclasses are not promoted to exact proofs.
> - `claim-details-v1` stores target-independent occurrence IDs, original source digests/byte spans, syntax, primary/secondary reasons, binding state, assumptions, alternatives and MRO provenance in evidence details. Python conclusions and tree-sitter syntax claims are distinct. Encoded/BOM/Unicode/CRLF round trips are tested; legacy snapshots remain unchanged.
> - Prominent Exact / Internal exact + candidates / All uncertainty presets use one shared JSON definition. The internal preset advertises and enforces internal endpoints; other filter flags remain intact. The browser test verifies actual expanded requests and saved-view restoration.

> ## Independent evidence
>
> The separate AST/symtable source oracle labels **182** same-file module-function calls under documented conservative exclusions. Baseline correct exact results: **76/182 (41.8%)**. New correct exact results: **177/182 (97.3%)**. No wrong-exact target was found in that labelled subset. These are positive supported-subset recall measurements, not whole-repository precision, whole-Python completeness or calibrated probabilities. Negative/unsupported cases have independent adversarial fixtures. See `scripts/phase5_oracle.py`, `output/phase5/independent-oracle.json` and the targeted eight-case source audit in `reports/P5_1_SOURCE_AUDIT.md`.
>
> ## Token-density evidence and scope
>
> The served format deliberately remains v1.0 during P5.1. A matched request shape and identical pinned source, using separately published Stage 3 analysis snapshots, produced:
>
> | Whole-envelope fixed response | Nodes | Relationships | o200k_base tokens | cl100k_base tokens |
> | :--- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
> | Pre-change v1.0 | {c['metrics']['nodes']} | {c['metrics']['relationships']} | {c['metrics']['full_envelope_tokens']} | {TokenCounter('cl100k_base').count(c['response'])} |
> | P5.1 v1.0 | {d['nodes']} | {d['relationships']} | {d['full_envelope_tokens']['o200k_base']} | {d['full_envelope_tokens']['cl100k_base']} |
>
> The actual requests use o200k_base with an 8,192-token limit. cl100k_base counts above are re-counts of those fixed envelopes, not a claim that an o200k request guarantees an 8,192 cl100k limit. Adaptive pages select different items after classification changes and BPE-dependent identity costs, so these rows do **not** isolate a schema density gain. Both use Stage 3 analysis, avoiding a false saving from missing Stage 1-only WL metadata.
>
> The separate fixed-envelope metadata ablation reduced 8,122 to 5,171 o200k tokens (36.3%) before adding useful descriptors/previews. The proposed 70% removable overhead, ~5x density and 60–90 tokens/item remain unproven goals. Actual v1.1 serializer/packing/round-trip comparisons belong to P5.2–P5.3; declarations alone do not realize those savings.
>

> ### What the remediation fixed
>
> **F1 (qualified paths)** is completely solved — all 12 answers now use `app/service.py.process` / `app/helpers.py.retry_save` in `paths[]`. The old run had 0/4 qualified names; this run has 12/12.
>
> ---
>
> ### Remaining failures, by cause
>
> **70B and 500B share the same two patterns:**
>
> - **direction/graph — unsupported citation:** 70B put `app/service.py.process` as the citation `path` (qualified name bled into the citation file field, oracle needs bare `app/service.py`). 500B used an extra vocabulary label `basis:source_control_flow` not in the oracle.
> - **stale_document both arms — missing `basis:source` fact:** Both models returned minimal 2-fact answers (`document:stale`, `immediate_callee:app/service.py.process`) but the oracle requires a third: `basis:source`. The fact-minimality instruction worked *too well* — it over-pruned.
> - **stale_document/graph — guide.md still absent:** The graph arm still doesn't deliver guide.md in evidence. Same retrieval gap as the Codex P5.5 pilot — this is upstream in the graph evidence selection, not a model issue.
>
> **300B (405B) failure is structural:**
>
> The 405B rejects `uniqueItems` in the JSON schema, so every call fell back to `json_object` mode. Without the schema enforcement, 405B filled `facts[]` with full prose sentences (`"app/service.py.process calls app/helpers.py.retry_save at line 8."`) rather than vocabulary labels — 5–6 unsupported facts per answer. The underlying knowledge was correct (paths right, citations right) but the vocabulary format was completely lost.
>
> **Fix:** strip `uniqueItems` from the strict schema (the rubric doesn't use it; it only caused the 400). Codex should add `uniqueItems` to the schema stripping list alongside the previous `maxItems`/`minimum` removals. That will keep 405B in json_schema mode and prevent the prose explosion.
>
> ---
>

> | 480B  | `["app/ui.py.submit", "app/ui.py.submit"]` (self-loop) | correct                                          |
>
> The graph arm **passes** for 405B and 480B because graph nodes explicitly encode the `ui.submit → service.process` edge. The flat arm only delivers app/ui.py source (which shows `return process(value)` — an imported function) plus `guide.md`. Without app/service.py in evidence, models infer the callee name from facts but fail to carry it into `paths`:
>
> - 70B grabbed the callee from the *direction* task (`helpers.retry_save`)
> - 405B latched onto the `bypass` function visible in app/ui.py line 12
> - 480B produced a self-loop
>
> **Fix options for Codex (pick one or combine):**
>
> 1. **Consistency rule** (cheapest): add an instruction — *“Every `immediate_callee:X` or `callee:X` label you select must appear verbatim as the final node in a `paths[][]` entry.”* Bridges the fact→path gap without changing evidence.
> 2. **Add app/service.py to flat evidence**: makes the callee’s declaration directly visible, removes the inference gap.
> 3. **Explicit path anchor in the question**: state “paths[] should run from app/ui.py.submit to its current immediate callee” so models know exactly what to write.
>
> Option 1 is the least-invasive contract change and would likely flip all three flat arm failures, since the models already know the right answer — they just don’t copy it into `paths`.

> The diagnosis is already above — here it is formatted for pasting directly to Codex:
>
> ---
>
> **Failure: `stale_document/flat_search` — `directed_paths_correct=False` across all 3 models**
>
> **What models return vs. oracle:**
>
> | Model      | `paths` returned                                         | Oracle                                             |
> | ---------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
> | Llama 70B  | `[["app/ui.py.submit", "app/helpers.py.retry_save"]]`    | `[["app/ui.py.submit", "app/service.py.process"]]` |
> | Llama 405B | `[["app/ui.py.submit", "app/ui.py.bypass"]]`             | same                                               |
> | Qwen 480B  | `[["app/ui.py.submit", "app/ui.py.submit"]]` (self-loop) | same                                               |
>
> **Root cause:** All 3 models correctly select `immediate_callee:app/service.py.process` in `facts`, but fail to carry that qualified name into `paths[][]`. The flat evidence includes app/ui.py (shows `return process(value)` at line 7, imported from `.service`) and `guide.md` — but NOT app/service.py. Without a graph edge or the callee’s source file, models infer the callee name but don’t use it in paths:
>
> - 70B confuses it with `helpers.retry_save` (the callee from the direction task)
> - 405B latches onto app/ui.py.bypass (a separate function at line 12 that calls storage.save)
> - 480B degenerates to a self-loop
>
> The graph arm passes for 405B and 480B because graph nodes explicitly encode the `ui.submit → service.process` directed edge, leaving no ambiguity.
>
> **Recommended fix (lowest friction):**
>
> Add a consistency rule to the contract:
>
> > *“Every `immediate_callee:X` or `callee:X` label you select in `facts` must appear verbatim as the final qualified name in at least one `paths[][]` entry.”*
>
> This requires no evidence changes and closes the fact→path gap directly. Models already know the right answer — they just don’t propagate it to `paths`.
>
> **Alternative (if the rule alone is insufficient):** Add app/service.py to the flat evidence (there are 3 source slots available, currently 2 used). That makes the callee’s declaration directly visible and removes the inference gap entirely."

> @tool()
> def get_related(title: str, limit: int = 8) -> dict:
>     """Rank distinct shared concepts with IDF weighting. Disclosure concepts excluded from ranking; shared terms do not establish scientific agreement."""
>     limit=_bound(limit,20)
>     with _conn() as conn:

>         result=[]
>         for key,v in sorted(scores.items(),key=lambda item:(-item[1]['score'],item[0]))[:limit]:
>             card=_card(conn.execute('SELECT * FROM read_documents WHERE id=?',(key,)).fetchone());card.update(score=round(v['score'],6),shared_concepts=len(v['shared']),shared_preview=v['shared'][:8]);result.append(card)
>         return dict(source='doc:'+str(did),related=result,ranking_basis='distinct_shared_concept_IDF_not_semantic_equivalence')

[Review and implementation report](./reports/CORPUS_AND_VIEWER_REMEDIATION_20261107.md) · [Installed Geo Grapher skill](C:/Users/jakem/.codex/skills/geo-grapher/SKILL.md)

> Here are some further fixes / improvements identified by claud : "" I'll mine the graph across the themes that matter for our system — dynamic/incremental updates, concept deduplication, retrieval strategy, and agent access patterns — then synthesize what's actionable.
>
> Two duplicate records turned up (two "Agentic AI" reviews and two "ReflectiChain" entries with different years), along with some abstracts that were never read; I'm pulling the retrieval and access-pattern themes next.
>
> Pulling claims from the eight most relevant papers.
>
> get_document returns about 4k tokens per paper with duplicated sections and noise concepts, which works against our token-efficiency goal. I'll fetch a few more papers before synthesizing.
>
> I have enough claims. Let me quickly quantify two structural problems the queries surfaced — concept fragmentation and duplicate documents — so the recommendations rest on numbers.
>
> I found two kinds of results: what the papers suggest we change, and what querying the graph showed about its own condition. The second matters most, so it comes first.
>
> ## The graph is barely connected
>
> | Metric | Value |
> |---|---|
> | Concepts shared by more than one paper | **52 of 1,098 (4.7%)** |
> | Papers that share no concept with any other | **70 of 200** |
> | Duplicate document records | 6 groups (several papers loaded twice: once from the `.md` note, once from the PDF) |
> | Abstracts that are just `<bottom line: unread>` | 43 |
> | Junk claims (headers like "Evidence the authors report:") | 129 |
> | Papers with no year | 74 |
>
> So `get_related` and `find_by_concept` mostly return noise. Every related-paper result I got had `shared_concepts: 1`. The cause is that the same idea gets stored under different names: "knowledge graph" / "knowledge graphs" / "knowledge graph kg", "genai" / "generative ai", "svd" / "truncated svd". On top of that, the concepts from the `.md` import are paper-specific labels like "trajectory cost and violation metrics", which no other paper will ever share.
>
> ## What the papers suggest
>
> **1. Merge duplicate concepts properly** (GraphRAG-ASCOC)
> - They remove duplicates caused by case, plurals and near-synonyms in two passes: an embedding search proposes candidate pairs, then an LLM confirms them.
> - Result: the vocabulary shrank to 83.6% of its original size while keeping 97.4% of the facts, with 0.94 precision when filling in missing links.
> - This is exactly our problem. Our `_normalise_concept()` only handles case and punctuation. An alias table, plus embedding-proposed pairs, plus a cheap 70B confirmation pass would likely connect most of those 70 isolated papers. We should also drop noise concepts like "competing financial interests" and "pytorch".
>
> **2. Return less, and rank it** (Mobile Agentic AI; ChatP&ID; Agentic AI review)
> - **More context isn't always better.** Too little knowledge forces the agent to keep searching; too much adds overlap it has to sort out. In their test, a moderate amount (K=3) worked best.
> - Right now `get_document` returns about 4k tokens, with 40+ unranked claims and duplicated sections. A `detail` parameter (`card` / `claims` / `full`), claims ranked by type, and a cap on how many come back would serve our token-efficiency goal directly.
> - **Find a starting point, then walk the graph.** ChatP&ID uses embeddings to find the entry node and the graph to expand from there. At a coarser graph level the input fell from 150K tokens to 32K, and ContextRAG had the best accuracy, cost and speed together.
> - Our search is plain `LIKE` matching. A search for "retrieval augmented generation" found nothing. SQLite's built-in FTS5 full-text index, with BM25 ranking, is a cheap first fix. The review warns that external memory stores often "return irrelevant or poorly ranked information".
>
> **3. Handle updates instead of rebuilding** (Mobile Agentic AI; CQRS)
> - **Sort knowledge by how long it lives.** Separate short-lived context from persistent knowledge, and sync once per knowledge update, not once per query. Applied here: hash each source file, re-extract only what changed, and keep cached per-topic "knowledge packs" that are invalidated when a source changes.
> - **Separate writing from reading (CQRS).** Keep the normalized SQLite tables as the write side, and build denormalized read views per tool (a document card, a concept card) whenever data is written. That way the store can grow and change without each query getting slower.
>
> **4. Let agents write their conclusions back** (Agentic AI review; Reflexion)
> - Both describe a loop where feedback updates memory. We could add an `annotate` tool so agents store what they work out across papers (contradictions, syntheses) with a note of where it came from. That is your stated goal: don't re-derive the same thing at query time.
>
> **5. Record where every claim came from** (KG.GOV)
> - Record origin on each claim: `md_import` or LLM, which model, extraction date, page. That lets us rank by trust and re-extract only the affected records when we upgrade a model.
>
> **6. Two smaller cautions**
> - **Treat paper text as untrusted** (Instruction Hierarchy). Claim text is third-party content that agents read through our tools. Tool descriptions should label it as data, not instructions.
> - **Embeddings go stale** (Semantic Drift Compensation). When we add embeddings, store the model version with each vector, and recompute concept averages when the model changes or the library grows.
>
> **How strong is the evidence?** The CQRS document and the Agentic AI review have no experiments. The K=3 result comes from one simulated drone setting. The ASCOC and ChatP&ID numbers are measured, but each on a single domain.
>
> ## Suggested order
>
> 1. Remove duplicate documents and junk claims. This is cheap and fixes skewed results now.
> 2. Merge concepts. This has the biggest effect on related-paper and concept lookups.
> 3. Add FTS5 search and `detail` levels on `get_document`. This is the token-efficiency win.
> 4. Add provenance columns, hash-based updates and read views. This is what handles a library that keeps changing.
> 5. Add agent write-back, then embeddings with versioning.
>
> I can also turn this into a shareable page if you want to pass it on.""

> Single-repository snapshots are already namespaced, but combined workspaces have a real isolation risk: import resolution shares a module index, and UI search sends no repository restriction. I’m making combined analysis isolated by default, with cross-repository resolution explicitly enabled. For the bounded pilot I’ll use Librarian_V2 and Timeline-mapping; the physics and CSI roots will get only a top-level assessment for now.

The system is now safer for **project-scoped code and Markdown navigation**, but it isn’t ready to fully represent all four mixed research/data repositories.

I found and fixed two blending risks: shared import/document-mention indexes and unrestricted cross-project search.

- Unrelated roots now use isolated namespaces by default.
- Each root appears as a separate sibling bubble cluster.
- Search and similarity require an explicit project scope; comparing all projects is deliberate.
- MCP discovery exposes project IDs, root IDs and ingestion coverage.

I tested **7 Librarian files and 8 Timeline notes**, rather than ingesting everything. The pilot produced zero cross-project relationships or documentary incidences. **172 tests passed**, plus browser and MCP checks.

The remaining gaps matter particularly for Quantule and network_management: native experiment datasets, PDFs, and explicit classifications for internal references, external sources, observations and hypotheses. Timeline content also remains documentary assertions—not automatically verified historical facts. The separate paper corpus still needs project-specific stores to prevent mixing.

[Open the two-cluster pilot](http://127.0.0.1:8773/) · [Full assessment and remaining work](./reports/PROJECT_ISOLATION_ASSESSMENT_20261107.md)

The new backend runs in the pilot. The existing 8765 server can load these changes after Claude’s current tests finish.

- Each project owns its store, aliases, search statistics, snapshots and handles.
- MCP queries require `graph_id`; federation is explicit.
- Root overlaps trigger review. Automatic merging is disabled.

> on the interactive panels can you add two features:
> 1) hold clicking the edge of a bubble allows you to expand it by scrolling the mouse wheel, or drag it to a new location.
> 2) in scenarios such as the one seen in the image showing the librarians graph, which shows teh documentation node off to the side,  and a long string of atomic nodes. Instead, when the node locations differ like this the orphaned node should scale appropriately to the amount of content held.
> 3) though i do like being able to see the graphs on different tiles, i think it would be better to instead adapt the main page's graph viewer to allow for the user to view all the graphs plotted in one window with each graph given its own space as to keep their seperation clear. this would allow the user to see and navigate between the different graphs. By creating a tool that lists the ingested graphs with a tick box style selection tool, the user can choose which graphs to display.
> i think the easiest pattern for per graph placement would be following a hexigon with the initial graph in the centre, and each additional graph taking up the space along a 5 sides.
> 4) drag to create a selection box to move or scale a group of nodes.

> @'
> import math,time
> from geographer.geometry import norm,violations,GOLDEN,GAP,PADDING
>
> def trial(rs):
>  n=len(rs);stride=max(1,int(n*.618))
>  while math.gcd(stride,n)>1:stride+=1
>  cs=[None]*n;placed=[]
>  for index,i in enumerate(sorted(range(n),key=lambda i:-rs[i])):
>   k=index*stride%n;z=1-2*(k+.5)/n;t=k*GOLDEN;v=(math.sqrt(1-z*z)*math.cos(t),math.sqrt(1-z*z)*math.sin(t),z)
>   intervals=[]
>   for j in placed:
>    c=cs[j];along=sum(a*b for a,b in zip(v,c));perp=max(0,sum(a*a for a in c)-along*along);r=rs[i]+rs[j]+GAP+.00001
>    if perp<r*r:
>     half=math.sqrt(r*r-perp)
>     if along+half>=0:intervals.append((along-half,along+half))
>   d=0.
>   for lo,hi in sorted(intervals):
>    if lo>d:break
>    if hi>=d:d=hi+.00001
>   cs[i]=tuple(a*d for a in v);placed.append(i)
>  R=PADDING+max(norm(c)+r for c,r in zip(cs,rs));return cs,R
> for rs in [[1.]*362,[.12,8,.6,1,3]*30,[1.]*3,[.2]*300+[2.,3.]]:
>  t=time.perf_counter();c,R=trial(rs);sp=[max(x[j] for x in c)-min(x[j] for x in c) for j in range(3)]
>  print(len(rs),R,violations(c,rs,R),sp,round(time.perf_counter()-t,3))
